1→"""Terminal rendering helpers for the `next` command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify import scoring as scoring_mod
     6→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_reporting_subjective import (
     7→    build_subjective_followup,
     8→)
     9→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.integrity import (
    11→    is_holistic_subjective_finding,
    12→    unassessed_subjective_dimensions,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.next_parts.render_support import (
    15→    is_auto_fix_command,
    16→    render_cluster_item as _render_cluster_item,
    17→    render_compact_item as _render_compact_item,
    18→    render_gate_banner,
    19→    render_grouped as _render_grouped,
    20→    render_queue_header,
    21→    scorecard_subjective,
    22→    show_empty_queue,
    23→    subjective_coverage_breakdown,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, log
    26→from desloppify.core.paths_api import read_code_snippet
    27→from desloppify.scoring import compute_health_breakdown, compute_score_impact
    28→
    29→def _render_item(
    30→    item: dict, dim_scores: dict, findings_scoped: dict, explain: bool,
    31→    potentials: dict | None = None,
    32→) -> None:
    33→    # Delegate to cluster renderer for cluster meta-items
    34→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    35→        _render_cluster_item(item)
    36→        return
    37→
    38→    # Workflow stage item (synthesis stages)
    39→    if item.get("kind") == "workflow_stage":
    40→        blocked = item.get("is_blocked", False)
    41→        stage = item.get("stage_name", "")
    42→        tag = " [blocked]" if blocked else ""
    43→        style = "dim" if blocked else "bold"
    44→        print(colorize(f"  (Planning stage: {stage}{tag})", style))
    45→        print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
    46→        print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
    47→        detail = item.get("detail", {})
    48→        total = detail.get("total_review_findings", 0)
    49→        if total:
    50→            print(colorize(f"  {total} review findings to analyze", "dim"))
    51→        if blocked:
    52→            blocked_by = item.get("blocked_by", [])
    53→            deps = ", ".join(b.replace("synthesis::", "") for b in blocked_by)
    54→            print(colorize(f"  Blocked by: {deps}", "dim"))
    55→            # Show the unblocking action
    56→            first_dep = blocked_by[0] if blocked_by else ""
    57→            dep_name = first_dep.replace("synthesis::", "")
    58→            if dep_name:
    59→                print(colorize(f"  Next step: desloppify plan synthesize --stage {dep_name}", "dim"))
    60→        else:
    61→            print(colorize(f"\n  Action: {item.get('primary_command', '')}", "cyan"))
    62→        return
    63→
    64→    # Workflow action item (e.g. create-plan)
    65→    if item.get("kind") == "workflow_action":
    66→        print(colorize("  (Workflow step)", "bold"))
    67→        print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
    68→        print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
    69→        print(colorize(f"\n  Action: {item.get('primary_command', '')}", "cyan"))
    70→        return
    71→
    72→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "medium")
    73→    print(colorize(f"  ({confidence} confidence)", "bold"))
    74→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
    75→    print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
    76→
    77→    # Effort/type label
    78→    if item.get("detector") == "review":
    79→        print(colorize("  Type: Design review (requires judgment)", "dim"))
    80→    elif is_auto_fix_command(item.get("primary_command")):
    81→        print(colorize("  Type: Auto-fixable", "dim"))
    82→
    83→    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
    84→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
    85→        detail = item.get("detail", {})
    86→        subjective_score = float(
    87→            detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    88→        )
    89→        print(f"  Dimension: {detail.get('dimension_name', 'unknown')}")
    90→        print(f"  Score: {subjective_score:.1f}%")
    91→        print(
    92→            colorize(
    93→                f"  Action: {item.get('primary_command', 'desloppify review --prepare')}",
    94→                "cyan",
    95→            )
    96→        )
    97→        print(colorize(
    98→            "  Note: re-review scores what it finds — scores can go down if issues are discovered.",
    99→            "dim",
   100→        ))
   101→        if explain:
   102→            reason = item.get("explain", {}).get(
   103→                "policy",
   104→                "subjective items sort after mechanical items at the same level.",
   105→            )
   106→            print(colorize(f"  explain: {reason}", "dim"))
   107→        return
   108→
   109→    # Plan overrides: description, cluster, note
   110→    if item.get("plan_description"):
   111→        print(colorize(f"  → {item['plan_description']}", "cyan"))
   112→    plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   113→    if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict):
   114→        cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "")
   115→        cluster_desc = plan_cluster.get("description") or ""
   116→        total = plan_cluster.get("total_items", 0)
   117→        desc_str = f' — "{cluster_desc}"' if cluster_desc else ""
   118→        print(colorize(f"  Cluster: {cluster_name}{desc_str} ({total} items)", "dim"))
   119→    if item.get("plan_note"):
   120→        print(colorize(f"  Note: {item['plan_note']}", "dim"))
   121→
   122→    print(f"  File: {item.get('file', '')}")
   123→    print(colorize(f"  ID:   {item.get('id', '')}", "dim"))
   124→
   125→    detail = item.get("detail", {})
   126→    if isinstance(detail, str):
   127→        detail = {"suggestion": detail}
   128→    if isinstance(detail, dict):
   129→        detail.setdefault("lines", [])
   130→        detail.setdefault("line", None)
   131→        detail.setdefault("category", None)
   132→        detail.setdefault("importers", None)
   133→        detail.setdefault("count", 0)
   134→    if detail.get("lines"):
   135→        print(f"  Lines: {', '.join(str(line_no) for line_no in detail['lines'][:8])}")
   136→    if detail.get("category"):
   137→        print(f"  Category: {detail['category']}")
   138→    if detail.get("importers") is not None:
   139→        print(f"  Active importers: {detail['importers']}")
   140→    if detail.get("suggestion"):
   141→        print(colorize(f"\n  Suggestion: {detail['suggestion']}", "dim"))
   142→
   143→    target_line = detail.get("line") or (detail.get("lines", [None]) or [None])[0]
   144→    if target_line and item.get("file") not in (".", ""):
   145→        snippet = read_code_snippet(item["file"], target_line)
   146→        if snippet:
   147→            print(colorize("\n  Code:", "dim"))
   148→            print(snippet)
   149→
   150→    if dim_scores:
   151→        detector = item.get("detector", "")
   152→        dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   153→        if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   154→            dimension_score = dim_scores[dimension.name]
   155→            strict_val = dimension_score.get("strict", dimension_score["score"])
   156→            print(
   157→                colorize(
   158→                    f"\n  Dimension: {dimension.name} — {dimension_score['score']:.1f}% "
   159→                    f"(strict: {strict_val:.1f}%) "
   160→                    f"({dimension_score['issues']} of {dimension_score['checks']:,} checks failing)",
   161→                    "dim",
   162→                )
   163→            )
   164→
   165→    # Score impact estimate
   166→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   167→    if potentials and detector and dim_scores:
   168→        try:
   169→            impact = compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, issues_to_fix=1)
   170→            if impact > 0:
   171→                print(colorize(f"  Impact: fixing this is worth ~+{impact:.1f} pts on overall score", "cyan"))
   172→            else:
   173→                # Single-item impact rounds to 0 — try bulk impact for all issues in detector
   174→                dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   175→                if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   176→                    issues = dim_scores[dimension.name].get("issues", 0)
   177→                    if issues > 1:
   178→                        bulk = compute_score_impact(dim_scores, potentials, detector, issues_to_fix=issues)
   179→                        if bulk > 0:
   180→                            print(colorize(
   181→                                f"  Impact: fixing all {issues} {detector} issues → ~+{bulk:.1f} pts",
   182→                                "cyan",
   183→                            ))
   184→        except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError) as exc:
   185→            log(f"  score impact estimate skipped: {exc}")
   186→    elif detector == "review" and dim_scores:
   187→        # Review findings: show dimension drag from breakdown
   188→        try:
   189→            dim_key = item.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")
   190→            if dim_key:
   191→                breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dim_scores)
   192→                for entry in breakdown.get("entries", []):
   193→                    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   194→                        continue
   195→                    entry_key = str(entry.get("name", "")).lower().replace(" ", "_")
   196→                    if entry_key == dim_key.lower().replace(" ", "_"):
   197→                        drag = float(entry.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0)
   198→                        if drag > 0.01:
   199→                            print(colorize(
   200→                                f"  Dimension drag: {entry['name']} costs -{drag:.2f} pts on overall score",
   201→                                "cyan",
   202→                            ))
   203→                        break
   204→        except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError) as exc:
   205→            log(f"  dimension drag estimate skipped: {exc}")
   206→
   207→    detector_name = item.get("detector", "")
   208→    auto_fix_command = item.get("primary_command")
   209→    if is_auto_fix_command(auto_fix_command):
   210→        similar_count = sum(
   211→            1
   212→            for finding in findings_scoped.values()
   213→            if finding.get("detector") == detector_name and finding["status"] == "open"
   214→        )
   215→        if similar_count > 1:
   216→            print(
   217→                colorize(
   218→                    f"\n  Auto-fixable: {similar_count} similar findings. "
   219→                    f"Run `{auto_fix_command}` to fix all at once.",
   220→                    "cyan",
   221→                )
   222→            )
   223→    if explain:
   224→        explanation = item.get("explain", {})
   225→        count_weight = explanation.get("count", int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0))
   226→        base = (
   227→            f"ranked by confidence={confidence}, "
   228→            f"count={count_weight}, id={item.get('id', '')}"
   229→        )
   230→
   231→        # Add dimension context for mechanical detectors
   232→        if dim_scores and detector:
   233→            dimension = scoring_mod.get_dimension_for_detector(detector)
   234→            if dimension and dimension.name in dim_scores:
   235→                ds = dim_scores[dimension.name]
   236→                base += (
   237→                    f". Dimension: {dimension.name} at {ds['score']:.1f}% "
   238→                    f"({ds['issues']} open issues)"
   239→                )
   240→
   241→        # For review findings, show subjective dimension context
   242→        if item.get("detector") == "review" and dim_scores:
   243→            dim_key = item.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")
   244→            if dim_key:
   245→                for ds_name, ds_data in dim_scores.items():
   246→                    if ds_name.lower().replace(" ", "_") == dim_key.lower().replace(" ", "_"):
   247→                        score_val = ds_data.get("score", "?")
   248→                        score_str = f"{score_val:.1f}" if isinstance(score_val, (int, float)) else str(score_val)
   249→                        base += f". Subjective dimension: {ds_name} at {score_str}%"
   250→                        break
   251→
   252→        policy = explanation.get("policy")
   253→        if policy:
   254→            base = f"{base}. {policy}"
   255→        print(colorize(f"  explain: {base}", "dim"))
   256→
   257→
   258→def render_terminal_items(
   259→    items: list[dict],
   260→    dim_scores: dict,
   261→    findings_scoped: dict,
   262→    *,
   263→    group: str,
   264→    explain: bool,
   265→    potentials: dict | None = None,
   266→    plan: dict | None = None,
   267→    cluster_filter: str | None = None,
   268→) -> None:
   269→    # Show focus header if plan has active cluster
   270→    if plan and plan.get("active_cluster"):
   271→        cluster_name = plan["active_cluster"]
   272→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   273→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
   274→        total = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   275→        print(colorize(f"\n  Focused on: {cluster_name} ({len(items)} of {total} remaining)", "cyan"))
   276→
   277→    if group != "item":
   278→        _render_grouped(items, group)
   279→        return
   280→
   281→    # Detect cluster drill-in: multiple items with cluster focus active
   282→    is_cluster_drill = len(items) > 1 and (
   283→        cluster_filter or (plan and plan.get("active_cluster"))
   284→    )
   285→
   286→    for idx, item in enumerate(items):
   287→        if idx > 0:
   288→            print()
   289→        # Full card for first item, compact for rest in cluster drill-in
   290→        if is_cluster_drill and idx > 0:
   291→            _render_compact_item(item, idx, len(items))
   292→            continue
   293→        queue_pos = item.get("queue_position")
   294→        if queue_pos and len(items) > 1:
   295→            label = f"  [#{queue_pos}]"
   296→        elif len(items) > 1:
   297→            label = f"  [{idx + 1}/{len(items)}]"
   298→        else:
   299→            pos_str = f"  (#{ queue_pos} in queue)" if queue_pos else ""
   300→            label = f"  Next item{pos_str}"
   301→        print(colorize(label, "bold"))
   302→        _render_item(item, dim_scores, findings_scoped, explain=explain, potentials=potentials)
   303→
   304→
   305→def render_single_item_resolution_hint(items: list[dict]) -> None:
   306→    if len(items) != 1:
   307→        return
   308→    kind = items[0].get("kind", "finding")
   309→    if kind in ("cluster", "workflow_stage", "workflow_action"):
   310→        return  # These kinds have their own resolution hints
   311→    if kind != "finding":
   312→        return
   313→    item = items[0]
   314→    detector_name = item.get("detector", "")
   315→    if detector_name == "subjective_review":
   316→        print(colorize("\n  Review with:", "dim"))
   317→        primary = item.get(
   318→            "primary_command", "desloppify show subjective"
   319→        )
   320→        print(f"    {primary}")
   321→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(item):
   322→            print("    desloppify review --prepare")
   323→        return
   324→
   325→    primary = item.get("primary_command", "")
   326→    if is_auto_fix_command(primary):
   327→        print(colorize("\n  Fix with:", "dim"))
   328→        print(f"    {primary}")
   329→        print(colorize("  Or resolve individually:", "dim"))
   330→    else:
   331→        print(colorize("\n  Resolve with:", "dim"))
   332→
   333→    print(
   334→        f'    desloppify plan done "{item["id"]}" --note "<what you did>" --confirm'
   335→    )
   336→    print(
   337→        f'    desloppify plan skip --permanent "{item["id"]}" --note "<why>" '
   338→        f'--attest "{ATTEST_EXAMPLE}"'
   339→    )
   340→
   341→
   342→def render_followup_nudges(
   343→    state: dict,
   344→    dim_scores: dict,
   345→    findings_scoped: dict,
   346→    *,
   347→    strict_score: float | None,
   348→    target_strict_score: float,
   349→    queue_total: int = 0,
   350→    plan_start_strict: float | None = None,
   351→    breakdown: "QueueBreakdown | None" = None,
   352→) -> None:
   353→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import (
   354→        format_queue_block,
   355→    )
   356→
   357→    subjective_threshold = target_strict_score
   358→    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
   359→    followup = build_subjective_followup(
   360→        state,
   361→        subjective_entries,
   362→        threshold=subjective_threshold,
   363→        max_quality_items=3,
   364→        max_integrity_items=5,
   365→    )
   366→    unassessed_subjective = unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)
   367→    # Show frozen plan-start score + queue block when in an active cycle
   368→    if queue_total > 0 and plan_start_strict is not None and breakdown is not None:
   369→        frozen = plan_start_strict
   370→        block = format_queue_block(breakdown, frozen_score=frozen)
   371→        print()
   372→        for text, style in block:
   373→            print(colorize(text, style))
   374→        print(colorize(
   375→            "  Score will not update until the queue is clear and you run `desloppify scan`.",
   376→            "dim",
   377→        ))
   378→    elif queue_total > 0 and plan_start_strict is not None:
   379→        print(
   380→            colorize(
   381→                f"\n  Score (frozen at plan start): strict {plan_start_strict:.1f}/100",
   382→                "cyan",
   383→            )
   384→        )
   385→        print(
   386→            colorize(
   387→                f"  Queue: {queue_total} item{'s' if queue_total != 1 else ''}"
   388→                " remaining. Score will not update until the queue is clear and you run `desloppify scan`.",
   389→                "dim",
   390→            )
   391→        )
   392→    elif strict_score is not None:
   393→        gap = round(float(target_strict_score) - float(strict_score), 1)
   394→        if gap > 0:
   395→            print(
   396→                colorize(
   397→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 → target {target_strict_score:.1f} (+{gap:.1f} needed)",
   398→                    "cyan",
   399→                )
   400→            )
   401→        else:
   402→            print(
   403→                colorize(
   404→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 meets target {target_strict_score:.1f}",
   405→                    "green",
   406→                )
   407→            )
   408→    # Show queue block after north star when no frozen score
   409→    if breakdown is not None and queue_total > 0 and plan_start_strict is None:
   410→        block = format_queue_block(breakdown)
   411→        for text, style in block:
   412→            print(colorize(text, style))
   413→
   414→    # Subjective bottleneck banner — only shown when the objective queue is
   415→    # clear.  While objective items remain, the queue is the single authority
   416→    # on what to work on next; no need to distract with subjective advice.
   417→    _objective_remaining = max(
   418→        0,
   419→        (breakdown.queue_total - breakdown.subjective) if breakdown else queue_total,
   420→    )
   421→    if strict_score is not None and dim_scores and _objective_remaining <= 0:
   422→        try:
   423→            health_breakdown = compute_health_breakdown(dim_scores)
   424→            subjective_drag = sum(
   425→                float(e.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0)
   426→                for e in health_breakdown.get("entries", [])
   427→                if isinstance(e, dict) and e.get("component") == "subjective"
   428→            )
   429→            mechanical_drag = sum(
   430→                float(e.get("overall_drag", 0) or 0)
   431→                for e in health_breakdown.get("entries", [])
   432→                if isinstance(e, dict) and e.get("component") != "subjective"
   433→            )
   434→            if subjective_drag > mechanical_drag and subjective_drag > 5.0:
   435→                print(colorize(
   436→                    f"\n  Subjective dimensions are the main bottleneck "
   437→                    f"(-{subjective_drag:.0f} pts vs -{mechanical_drag:.0f} pts mechanical).",
   438→                    "yellow",
   439→                ))
   440→                print(colorize(
   441→                    "  Code fixes alone won't close the gap — run "
   442→                    "`desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` "
   443→                    "to re-score.",
   444→                    "yellow",
   445→                ))
   446→        except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError, KeyError) as exc:
   447→            log(f"  subjective bottleneck banner skipped: {exc}")
   448→
   449→    # Integrity penalty/warn lines preserved (anti-gaming safeguard, must remain visible).
   450→    for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
   451→        print(colorize(f"\n  {message}", style))
   452→
   453→    # Rescan nudge after structural work
   454→    if queue_total > 10:
   455→        print(colorize(
   456→            "\n  Tip: after structural fixes (splitting files, moving code), rescan to "
   457→            "let cascade effects settle: `desloppify scan --path .`",
   458→            "dim",
   459→        ))
   460→
   461→    # Collapsed subjective summary.
   462→    coverage_open, _coverage_reasons, _holistic_reasons = subjective_coverage_breakdown(
   463→        findings_scoped
   464→    )
   465→    parts: list[str] = []
   466→    low_dims = len(followup.low_assessed)
   467→    unassessed_count = len(unassessed_subjective)
   468→    stale_count = sum(1 for e in subjective_entries if e.get("stale"))
   469→    open_review = [
   470→        f for f in findings_scoped.values()
   471→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
   472→    ]
   473→    if low_dims:
   474→        parts.append(f"{low_dims} dimension{'s' if low_dims != 1 else ''} below target")
   475→    if stale_count:
   476→        parts.append(f"{stale_count} stale")
   477→    if unassessed_count:
   478→        parts.append(f"{unassessed_count} unassessed")
   479→    if len(open_review):
   480→        parts.append(f"{len(open_review)} review finding{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open")
   481→    if coverage_open > 0:
   482→        parts.append(f"{coverage_open} file{'s' if coverage_open != 1 else ''} need review")
   483→
   484→    if parts:
   485→        print(colorize(f"\n  Subjective: {', '.join(parts)}.", "cyan"))
   486→        print(colorize("  Run `desloppify show subjective` for details.", "dim"))
   487→
   488→
   489→__all__ = [
   490→    "is_auto_fix_command",
   491→    "render_followup_nudges",
   492→    "render_gate_banner",
   493→    "render_queue_header",
   494→    "render_single_item_resolution_hint",
   495→    "render_terminal_items",
   496→    "scorecard_subjective",
   497→    "show_empty_queue",
   498→    "subjective_coverage_breakdown",
   499→]
   500→

    20→@dataclass
    21→class SynthesisGuardrailResult:
    22→    """Structured result from synthesis staleness detection."""
    23→
    24→    is_stale: bool = False
    25→    new_ids: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)

    28→
    29→def synthesis_guardrail_status(
    30→    *,
    31→    plan: dict | None = None,
    32→    state: dict | None = None,
    33→) -> SynthesisGuardrailResult:
    34→    """Pure detection: is synthesis stale? Returns structured result, no side effects."""
    35→    try:
    36→        resolved_plan = plan if isinstance(plan, dict) else load_plan()
    37→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
    38→        return SynthesisGuardrailResult()
    39→

    41→
    42→    if not is_synthesis_stale(resolved_plan, resolved_state):
    43→        return SynthesisGuardrailResult(_plan=resolved_plan)
    44→
    45→    new_ids: set[str] = set()
    46→    if resolved_state:
    47→        new_ids = compute_new_finding_ids(resolved_plan, resolved_state)
    48→
    49→    return SynthesisGuardrailResult(is_stale=True, new_ids=new_ids, _plan=resolved_plan)

    51→

    73→    return True

Now I have a complete understanding of the entire pipeline. Here is the diagnosis.

---

## Diagnosis

### The Root Cause: `apply_plan_order` overrides `item_sort_key` ordering for synthesis items

The bug is a two-stage problem created by the interaction between the initial sort and the plan-order rewrite.

**Stage 1 -- `item_sort_key` sorts correctly.** In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py`, lines 89-92:

```python
# Synthesis stage items: tier -2, stage order, blocked after unblocked
if kind == "workflow_stage":
    blocked_penalty = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
    stage_index = int(item.get("stage_index", 0))
    return (-2, blocked_penalty, stage_index, item.get("id", ""))
```

This produces sort keys:
- `observe` (unblocked): `(-2, 0, 0, "synthesis::observe")`
- `reflect` (blocked): `(-2, 1, 1, "synthesis::reflect")`
- `organize` (blocked): `(-2, 1, 2, "synthesis::organize")`
- `commit` (blocked): `(-2, 1, 3, "synthesis::commit")`

This is **correct** -- `observe` sorts first because `blocked_penalty=0` comes before `blocked_penalty=1`.

**Stage 2 -- `apply_plan_order` destroys the correct ordering.** In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py`, lines 60-82:

```python
ordered: list[dict] = []
ordered_ids: set[str] = set()
for finding_id in queue_order:
    if finding_id in by_id and finding_id not in skipped_ids:
        ordered.append(by_id[finding_id])
        ordered_ids.add(finding_id)

skipped_items: list[dict] = []
remaining_existing: list[dict] = []
remaining_new: list[dict] = []
_new = new_ids or set()
for item in items:
    item_id = item["id"]
    if item_id in ordered_ids:
        continue
    if item_id in skipped_ids:
        skipped_items.append(item)
    elif item_id in _new:
        remaining_new.append(item)
    else:
        remaining_existing.append(item)

result = ordered + remaining_existing + remaining_new
```

The `queue_order` list in `plan.json` contains the synthesis stage IDs in their **dependency order**: `["synthesis::observe", "synthesis::reflect", "synthesis::organize", "synthesis::commit", ...]`. But the critical detail is that `apply_plan_order` emits items in **exactly the order they appear in `queue_order`** -- it does not re-sort within that ordered segment by blocked status.

However, examining this more carefully, the `queue_order` should list `observe` first if the IDs were inserted in order. The real issue becomes clear when you look at how `queue_order` is typically populated -- the IDs are inserted in order `observe, reflect, organize, commit`. So at first glance this should be fine.

Let me re-examine. The `queue_order` preserves insertion order, and after `apply_plan_order`, all 4 synthesis items are in the `ordered` list in `queue_order` order. So the order should be `observe, reflect, organize, commit`. That seems correct...

Let me look more carefully at whether there is a re-sort happening *after* `apply_plan_order`.

Looking back at `core.py` lines 167-199:

```python
all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)           # Line 167 — correct sort

# Apply living plan ordering if provided    
if resolved_options.plan:                    # Line 171
    all_items = _apply_plan_order(...)       # Line 173 — preserves queue_order

# Collapse auto-clusters                    
if should_collapse:                          # Line 188
    all_items = _collapse_clusters(...)      # Line 189 — re-sorts!

# Interleave subjective                     
if should_interleave:                        # Line 198
    all_items = _apply_subjective_interleave_guardrail(all_items)  # Line 199
```

The interleave guardrail at lines 80-94 is the smoking gun:

```python
workflow_items: list[dict] = []
subjective_items: deque[dict] = deque()
objective_items: deque[dict] = deque()
for item in items:
    if item.get("kind") in _WORKFLOW_KINDS:
        workflow_items.append(item)
    elif _is_subjective_queue_item(item):
        subjective_items.append(item)
    else:
        objective_items.append(item)

if not subjective_items or not objective_items:
    return items
```

Wait -- this early-returns `items` unchanged if there are no subjective OR no objective items. If both exist, it does:

```python
interleaved: list[dict] = list(workflow_items)
```

This preserves the order of `workflow_items` as they were extracted from `items`. Since `apply_plan_order` put them in `queue_order` order (observe, reflect, organize, commit), the interleave should preserve that. So the interleave is not the problem either.

Let me re-examine `apply_plan_order` more carefully -- specifically, what happens when the `queue_order` itself has `commit` before `observe`.

Now let me take a careful look at all the output to form my critique.

---

## Detailed UX Critique

### A) Naming: Is "plan synthesize" intuitive?

No. As a first-time agent encountering this tool, "plan synthesize" is not intuitive at all. The word "synthesize" is academic and vague -- synthesize *what*? It does not communicate the actual action: triaging review findings, spotting contradictions, clustering them, and writing a strategy. The internal stages (observe, strategize, organize, commit) are also non-obvious -- they sound like a meditation exercise, not a code quality workflow.

What I would have expected this to be called, and my alternative suggestions:

1. **`plan triage`** -- This is the best option. "Triage" is universally understood in engineering as "sort incoming items by priority and decide what to do with each." That is exactly what this workflow does: look at review findings, identify contradictions, cluster by root cause, prioritize. Every developer and every AI agent knows what triage means.

2. **`plan review`** -- Simple and direct. You're reviewing the review findings. Downside: name collision with the `review` command itself could cause confusion.

3. **`plan analyze`** -- Communicates the "look at findings and figure out what matters" step. Less precise than triage but better than synthesize.

4. **`plan digest`** -- Implies compressing many inputs into a summary. Somewhat literary but more concrete than synthesize.

5. **`plan classify`** -- Describes the clustering/categorization action. Too narrow though -- it misses the contradiction-spotting and strategy-writing parts.

**My recommendation: `plan triage`**. It is the most immediately understood term for "sort through a pile of incoming items, decide what matters, group them, and make a plan." It is also a verb that implies action with a clear end state, which "synthesize" does not.

### B) Discoverability: Is it obvious from every surface that you're in plan mode?

**`next` -- Rating: 2/5**

The `next` command shows "(Planning stage: commit [blocked])" and "Planning mode needed" at the top. But the main body of the output is deeply confusing. It says:

> Next item (#1 in queue)
> (Planning stage: commit [blocked])
> Planning: Write strategy & confirm
> 2 review findings to analyze
> Blocked by: organize
> Next step: desloppify plan synthesize --stage organize

This is a mess. The "next item" is supposedly #1 in the queue, but it is *blocked* and you cannot do it. The actual actionable next step (`--stage observe`) is buried. The banner at the top says "run: desloppify plan synthesize" which is correct but generic. An agent reading this would see "Next item (#1)" and "blocked" and not know what to do -- the signal-to-noise ratio is terrible. The one-line banner at the top is easy to miss.

**`plan synthesize` -- Rating: 4/5**

This is actually the clearest surface. The stage list with arrows (current) and circles (future) is good. The "Next step" section gives you the exact command to run. The findings are grouped by dimension. It tells you what to look for (contradictions). This is the only surface that feels like it was designed for the user currently in this state. One point off because the command name itself is confusing (see section A).

**`status` -- Rating: 1/5**

This is the worst offender. The single-line banner "Planning mode needed -- review findings require analysis before fixing" appears at the very top, and then the command dumps an ENORMOUS wall of output: scores, dimension tables, structural debt tables, score recipes, agent plans. An agent reading `status` would get buried in all that data and likely start working on the 535 security findings or the structural debt areas, completely ignoring the planning mode banner. The "AGENT PLAN" section at the very bottom says "Living plan active" but does not explicitly say "YOU ARE IN PLAN MODE -- DO NOT START FIXING THINGS YET." The word "planning" appears in the queue count "(4 planning steps)" but that is easy to gloss over.

**`plan queue` -- Rating: 2/5**

The banner at the top is the same one-liner. The queue itself shows items 1-4 as "planning" detector type, but they look identical to every other queue item. There is no visual separation, no box, no header that says "=== PLANNING MODE: Complete these 4 steps first ===". They are just rows in a table. An agent scanning the queue would see items 1-4, think "ok those are planning items," then look at item 5 and say "that looks actionable, let me fix that bare except clause." Nothing stops it.

### C) Confusion Points

1. **The banner is too quiet.** "Planning mode needed -- review findings require analysis before fixing. Run: desloppify plan synthesize" appears as a single indented line that looks like every other informational line. It should be loud. A box. Color. ALL CAPS. Something. In a wall of output, a single quiet line is invisible.

2. **`next` shows you a blocked item as the "next" item.** This is the single most confusing thing. If something is blocked, it is by definition not "next." The command should show you the thing you should actually DO next, not the thing at position #1 that you cannot do.

3. **The stage names are opaque.** "observe", "strategize", "organize", "commit" -- these are not self-documenting. The `plan synthesize` dashboard helpfully expands them ("Analyse findings & spot contradictions") but everywhere else you just see the short names. An agent seeing "Blocked by: organize" has no idea what "organize" means without context.

4. **582 items in the queue creates panic.** An agent sees "Queue: 582 items" and thinks the codebase is a disaster. But most of those are low-priority mechanical findings. The planning mode is about 2 review findings. The 582 number dominates the output and creates a misleading sense of scale for what you're actually supposed to do right now.

5. **The `status` command's structural debt section is actively misleading during plan mode.** It says "Create a task doc for each area -> farm to sub-agents for decomposition" which sounds like an instruction to go do that right now. An agent would read that and start creating task docs, completely bypassing the planning mode workflow.

6. **No clear mental model.** There is no single place that explains: "You are in planning mode. This means review findings need to be analyzed before you fix code. There are 4 stages. You are on stage 1. Here is what each stage does. When you finish all 4, you exit planning mode and can start fixing things." The information is scattered across surfaces in fragments.

### D) The Blocked Item Problem

This is seriously confusing. Here is what `next` shows:

```
Next item  (#1 in queue)
(Planning stage: commit [blocked])
Planning: Write strategy & confirm
Blocked by: organize
Next step: desloppify plan synthesize --stage organize
```

The queue order is inverted. Item #1 is "commit" (the LAST stage), and it is blocked. Items #2, #3, #4 are the earlier stages. The "Next step" line says `--stage organize`, but the actual first step you should do is `--stage observe`. So the `next` command is showing you:
- The wrong item (the last stage instead of the first)
- The wrong next step (organize instead of observe)
- A "blocked" label that makes you feel stuck

What it SHOULD show:

```
  ┌─ PLANNING MODE ─────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ 2 review findings need analysis before fixing begins.   │
  │ Stage 1 of 4: Analyse findings & spot contradictions    │
  │                                                         │
  │ Run: desloppify plan synthesize                         │
  │   (this will show you the findings and instructions)    │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

That is: show the FIRST actionable stage, not the last blocked one. Do not mention blocked items at all. Give the user the one command they need to run. The `plan synthesize` dashboard is actually good at showing the stages -- just get the user there.

### E) Exit Clarity

No, it is not obvious how to get out of plan mode. Across all four surfaces I ran:

- `next` does not mention how to exit or how many stages remain
- `plan synthesize` shows the 4-stage list which implies finishing all 4 will do something, but does not explicitly say "after stage 4, planning mode ends and you return to normal fixing"
- `status` does not mention it at all
- `plan queue` does not mention it

An agent would reasonably ask: "If I don't want to do this planning stuff, can I skip it? What if I just want to fix the bare except clause directly?" There is no guidance on that. There is no `plan exit` or `plan skip-all` or `plan complete` mentioned anywhere. The only way I can infer to exit is to complete all 4 stages or manually skip/defer the planning items, but that is not stated anywhere.

**What should exist:** Every surface that shows the planning mode banner should also show: "To skip planning: `desloppify plan skip-synthesis`" or "Complete all 4 stages to exit planning mode and resume normal queue."

---

### Summary

The core workflow (`plan synthesize`) is actually well-designed once you get to it. The 4-stage pipeline with the dashboard is clear and useful. The problem is **getting there**. Every other surface (`next`, `status`, `plan queue`) does a poor job of:
1. Making it visually obvious you are in a special mode
2. Telling you what to do (the one command to run)
3. Not burying the signal under mountains of unrelated output
4. Showing the correct next action (not a blocked item)

The single biggest fix would be: when in planning mode, `next` should show ONLY the planning mode information with the command to run, and suppress all the normal queue/score/tip output. The user does not need to see the north star score or the 582-item queue count or a tip about rescanning. They need to see: "You are in planning mode. Run this command. Here is why."

> AGENT

OK so the injection order is correct. Let me now think about this differently. Perhaps the issue is not in the ordering at all, but in how `next` picks what to display. Let me re-read the scenario more carefully.

The user says `next` shows:
```
Next item  (#1 in queue)
(Planning stage: commit [blocked])
```

So `commit` is literally position #1 in the queue. That means `apply_plan_order` put `commit` first. This would happen if `queue_order` has `commit` listed before the other stages. Let me check if there's a scenario where the queue_order can get reversed.

Looking at `sync_synthesis_needed` again (line 338-346):

```python
if current_hash and current_hash != last_hash and not already_present:
    insert_at = _after_promoted(order, plan)
    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "commit")
    existing = set(order)
    injected_count = 0
    for sid, name in zip(SYNTHESIS_STAGE_IDS, stage_names):
        if name not in confirmed and sid not in existing:
            order.insert(insert_at + injected_count, sid)
            injected_count += 1
```

This inserts in the correct order. BUT -- what if stages were already present before (from a previous injection), and only some got confirmed? The check at line 332 says `already_present = any(sid in order for sid in SYNTHESIS_IDS)`, so if ANY stage is in the queue, it will NOT re-inject. That means the existing order in `queue_order` is preserved.

But here's the real key insight: **`apply_plan_order` treats `queue_order` as the single source of truth for ordering**. The items come out in whatever order they appear in `queue_order`. The `item_sort_key` sort on line 167 of `core.py` happens BEFORE `apply_plan_order` on line 173, and then `apply_plan_order` REPLACES that ordering with the `queue_order` ordering.

So if through any mutation of the plan (manual reordering, reconciliation, etc.) the `queue_order` ends up with `commit` before `observe`, the correct `item_sort_key` ordering is thrown away.

Let me check the reconcile module for any reordering.

    25→def _find_candidates(

    46→def _supersede_id(

   107→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   108→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   109→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   110→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   111→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   112→
   113→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   114→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   115→        try:
   116→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   117→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   118→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   119→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   120→                to_prune.append(fid)
   121→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   122→            to_prune.append(fid)
   123→
   124→    for fid in to_prune:
   125→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   126→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   127→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   128→
   129→    return to_prune
   130→
   131→
   132→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   133→    plan: PlanModel,
   134→    state: StateModel,
   135→) -> ReconcileResult:
   136→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   137→
   138→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   139→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   140→    """
   141→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   142→    result = ReconcileResult()
   143→    now = utc_now()
   144→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   145→
   146→    # Collect all finding IDs referenced by the plan
   147→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   148→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   149→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   150→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   151→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   152→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   153→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   154→
   155→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   156→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   157→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   158→    referenced_ids = {
   159→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   160→        if not any(fid.startswith(prefix) for prefix in SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES)
   161→    }
   162→
   163→    # Check each referenced ID
   164→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   165→        if not _is_finding_alive(state, fid):
   166→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   167→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   168→                result.changes += 1
   169→
   170→    # Reconcile epic clusters: remove dead findings, delete empty epics
   171→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   172→    epic_names_to_delete: list[str] = []
   173→    for name, cluster in list(clusters.items()):
   174→        if not name.startswith(EPIC_PREFIX):
   175→            continue
   176→        finding_ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   177→        alive_ids = [fid for fid in finding_ids if _is_finding_alive(state, fid)]
   178→        if alive_ids != finding_ids:
   179→            cluster["finding_ids"] = alive_ids
   180→            result.changes += 1
   181→        if not alive_ids:
   182→            epic_names_to_delete.append(name)
   183→    for name in epic_names_to_delete:
   184→        clusters.pop(name, None)
   185→        result.changes += 1
   186→
   187→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   188→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   189→    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import resurface_stale_skips
   190→
   191→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   192→    if resurfaced:
   193→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   194→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   195→
   196→    # Prune old superseded entries
   197→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   198→    result.pruned = pruned
   199→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   200→
   201→    # Log reconciliation if any changes were made
   202→    if result.changes > 0:
   203→        from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import append_log_entry
   204→
   205→        append_log_entry(
   206→            plan,
   207→            "reconcile",
   208→            finding_ids=result.superseded,
   209→            actor="system",
   210→            detail={
   211→                "superseded_count": len(result.superseded),
   212→                "pruned_count": len(result.pruned),
   213→                "resurfaced_count": len(result.resurfaced),
   214→            },
   215→        )
   216→
   217→    return result
   218→
   219→
   220→@dataclass
   221→class ReviewImportSyncResult:
   222→    """Summary of plan changes after a review import."""
   223→
   224→    new_ids: set[str]
   225→    added_to_queue: list[str]
   226→    synthesis_injected: bool
   227→
   228→
   229→def sync_plan_after_review_import(
   230→    plan: PlanModel,
   231→    state: StateModel,
   232→) -> ReviewImportSyncResult | None:
   233→    """Sync plan queue after review import. Pure engine function — no I/O.
   234→
   235→    Appends new finding IDs to queue_order and injects synthesis::pending
   236→    if needed. Returns None when there are no new findings to sync.
   237→    """
   238→    from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
   239→        compute_new_finding_ids,
   240→        sync_synthesis_needed,
   241→    )
   242→
   243→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   244→    new_ids = compute_new_finding_ids(plan, state)
   245→    if not new_ids:
   246→        return None
   247→
   248→    # Add new finding IDs to end of queue_order so they have position
   249→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   250→    existing = set(order)
   251→    added: list[str] = []
   252→    for fid in sorted(new_ids):
   253→        if fid not in existing:
   254→            order.append(fid)
   255→            added.append(fid)
   256→
   257→    # Inject synthesis::pending if needed
   258→    synthesis_result = sync_synthesis_needed(plan, state)
   259→    synthesis_injected = bool(
   260→        synthesis_result and getattr(synthesis_result, "injected", False)
   261→    )
   262→
   263→    return ReviewImportSyncResult(
   264→        new_ids=new_ids,
   265→        added_to_queue=added,
   266→        synthesis_injected=synthesis_injected,
   267→    )
   268→
   269→
   270→__all__ = [
   271→    "ReconcileResult",
   272→    "ReviewImportSyncResult",
   273→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   274→    "sync_plan_after_review_import",
   275→]
   276→
```

Now I have a thorough understanding. Here is my analysis.

---

## Analysis of SKILL.md from an Agent's Perspective: Plan Synthesis

### 1. Phase 2 (Plan): Does SKILL.md clearly explain what "plan synthesize" is and when to use it?

**Short answer: No.** The section introduces the commands but never explains the *purpose* or *trigger conditions* of the synthesis workflow.

Here is the complete Phase 2 text:

> After reviews, synthesis stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`. Complete them in order:
> ```
> desloppify plan synthesize --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
> desloppify plan synthesize --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
> desloppify plan synthesize --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
> desloppify plan synthesize --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
> ```

**Problems:**

**(a) No definition of what "synthesis" means here.** The word appears out of nowhere. An agent reading this for the first time would see "synthesis stages and plan creation appear as queue items" and have no mental model for what a "synthesis stage" is or why it exists. The code reveals that synthesis is a 4-stage deliberation process (OBSERVE, REFLECT, ORGANIZE, COMMIT) where the agent must analyze scan/review findings, compare against prior work, organize clusters, and commit to a strategy. None of this is explained in SKILL.md.

**(b) No explanation of WHEN synthesis is required.** The sentence "After reviews, synthesis stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`" implies it happens automatically after reviews, but there are actually multiple trigger conditions:
- First time after a review (no prior synthesis exists)
- When synthesis becomes "stale" (new review findings appear that haven't been triaged)
- After a rescan that surfaces new findings

The guardrails code shows that `next` can actually **block** the agent from resolving items when synthesis is stale (`require_synthesis_current_or_exit` calls `sys.exit(1)`). An agent encountering that for the first time would not understand why it's being blocked or what to do, because SKILL.md never mentions this guardrail.

**(c) No explanation of the `--confirm-existing` fast path.** The code has an entire alternate flow (`_cmd_confirm_existing`) for when the agent has already done synthesis once and just needs to confirm the existing plan is still valid. This is surfaced in the tool's own output but never mentioned in SKILL.md. An agent would see the tool suggest `--confirm-existing` and have no documentation context for it.

**(d) The `--report` values are placeholder gibberish.** The example shows `--report "themes and root causes..."` with literal ellipsis. An agent does not know:
- How long the report needs to be (code reveals: 100 chars minimum for observe/reflect, structural requirements for organize)
- What the report should actually contain
- That the tool provides a `build_synthesis_prompt` function that generates guidance

The playbook file reveals the actual stage purposes:
- observe: "Analyse findings & spot contradictions"
- reflect: "Form strategy & present to user"
- organize: "Defer contradictions, cluster, & prioritize"
- commit: "Write strategy & confirm"

None of this appears in SKILL.md.

### 2. Does the name "synthesize" make sense?

**It is confusing.** Here is why:

The word "synthesize" in common usage means "to combine multiple things into a coherent whole." In chemistry it means to create a compound. In academic writing it means to merge sources into a unified argument. These connotations are reasonable for what the feature does -- combining scan findings, review results, and prior history into a plan -- but the problem is that **the name doesn't describe the agent's action, it describes the system's interpretation of the action.**

An agent reading `plan synthesize --stage observe` has to parse:
1. `plan` -- OK, I'm working with the plan
2. `synthesize` -- am I creating something? combining something? what am I synthesizing?
3. `--stage observe` -- wait, now there are stages within synthesis? what's the mental model?

**Evaluation of alternatives:**

- **`plan mode` / `plan enter`**: Clear that you're entering a distinct operational mode with stages. "Enter plan mode" is immediately understandable. But it doesn't convey that there's analytical work happening.

- **`plan analyze`**: Better than "synthesize" because the core action IS analysis -- the agent is analyzing findings. But it loses the sense of "building toward something." Would work well for the observe/reflect stages but poorly for organize/commit.

- **`plan build`**: Implies constructing the plan, which is accurate. `plan build --stage observe` reads well. But it understates the deliberation -- an agent might think it's a mechanical operation.

- **`plan prepare`**: This is arguably the best fit. The entire synthesis workflow is preparation for execution. `plan prepare --stage observe` reads naturally. "Preparation" implies multiple steps leading to readiness. The downside: it could be confused with `review --prepare`.

- **`plan review`**: Terrible idea. There's already a top-level `review` command for subjective scoring. Using "review" again would create constant confusion about which review is meant.

**My recommendation: `plan prepare` or `plan build`.** Both convey "you're constructing the plan through deliberate stages" more clearly than "synthesize," which sounds academic and vague. `plan prepare` has the slight edge because the entire point is preparing to execute.

However, the name matters less than the documentation. Even "synthesize" would work fine if SKILL.md explained it properly.

### 3. Flow Clarity: Can an agent understand the full lifecycle?

**Partially, but with significant gaps.**

The high-level flow in Section 2 is clear:
> scan -> plan -> execute -> rescan (cycle)

The three phases are well-structured. But the **transitions between phases** are poorly explained.

**What's clear:**
- Phase 1 instructions are concrete: run `scan`, check `status`, run `review` if told to.
- Phase 3 is excellent: "Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue." The numbered loop (next -> fix -> resolve -> repeat) is exactly the right level of specificity.
- The instruction "When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1" gives a clear cycle boundary.

**What's unclear:**

**(a) The transition from Phase 1 to Phase 2.** The document says:
> After reviews, synthesis stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`.

This is passive voice and vague. An agent doesn't know: Do I run something to trigger this? Does it happen automatically? What if I haven't done a review -- do I still need to synthesize? What if I'm on my second scan cycle and the plan already exists?

**(b) The transition from Phase 2 to Phase 3.** After the synthesis commands, there's a block about shaping the queue (move, cluster, focus, skip). But there's no explicit signal for "you're done planning, start executing." An agent doesn't know: Am I done after `--complete`? Do I need to also run `plan move` commands? Is shaping optional?

**(c) The `next` command serves double duty but this isn't explained.** In Phase 2, `next` shows synthesis stages as queue items. In Phase 3, `next` shows actual findings to fix. The document doesn't explain that `next` is the universal "what do I do now" oracle that adapts its output based on plan state. An agent might try to skip to Phase 3 and be confused when `next` shows synthesis stages instead of code issues.

**(d) The guardrail system is invisible.** The code shows that resolving findings is **blocked** when synthesis is stale. This is a hard exit(1). An agent will be surprised by this if SKILL.md doesn't mention it.

### 4. What's Missing

**Critical omissions:**

1. **A one-sentence definition of synthesis at the top of Phase 2.** Something like: "Synthesis is a structured analysis of your scan and review findings. It forces you to understand what's wrong before deciding what to fix. You must complete it before the tool will let you resolve findings."

2. **Trigger conditions.** When does synthesis appear? First scan after review? Every scan? Only when new findings appear? The agent needs to know this.

3. **The confirm-existing path.** On subsequent cycles, the agent can fast-track with `--confirm-existing` if the plan is still valid. This is a major time-saver that the document omits entirely.

4. **Stage requirements.** What does each stage expect? The code has minimum character lengths, structural requirements (clusters must have descriptions and action_steps for organize), and dependency ordering. None of this is documented.

5. **The guardrail/blocking behavior.** The agent needs to know that skipping synthesis will hard-block `resolve` commands.

6. **What the `--report` argument should contain.** The placeholder strings are not helpful. Even a one-line description per stage would help: "observe: describe the main themes and root causes you see in the findings" etc.

7. **The `build_synthesis_prompt` helper.** The engine has a function that generates a detailed prompt for each stage based on current findings. If an agent knew about this, it could use it to produce better reports.

**Structural suggestions:**

Phase 2 should be split into two visually distinct sections:
- **2a: Synthesis (required analysis)** -- the observe/reflect/organize/commit flow with clear explanations
- **2b: Queue shaping (optional optimization)** -- the move/cluster/focus/skip commands

This separation would make it clear that synthesis is mandatory but queue shaping is discretionary.

### Summary

SKILL.md's Phase 2 is the weakest section of an otherwise well-structured document. The synthesis workflow is a 4-stage deliberation process with guardrails, fast-track paths, minimum requirements, and blocking behavior -- and the document reduces it to four example commands with placeholder arguments. An agent encountering synthesis for the first time would not understand what it is, why it exists, when it triggers, what it requires, or how to handle the second-cycle fast-track path. The name "synthesize" adds a layer of ambiguity that better alternatives like "prepare" or "build" would avoid, but the documentation gap is more impactful than the naming.

    99→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
   100→    return effective_cluster
   101→
   102→
   103→def _build_next_payload(
   104→    *,
   105→    queue: dict,
   106→    items: list[dict],
   107→    state: dict,
   108→    narrative: dict,
   109→    plan_data: dict | None,
   110→) -> dict:
   111→    payload = next_output_mod.build_query_payload(
   112→        queue, items, command="next", narrative=narrative, plan=plan_data
   113→    )
   114→    payload["overall_score"] = state_mod.get_overall_score(state)
   115→    payload["objective_score"] = state_mod.get_objective_score(state)
   116→    payload["strict_score"] = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   117→    payload["scorecard_dimensions"] = scorecard_dimensions_payload(
   118→        state,
   119→        dim_scores=state.get("dimension_scores", {}),
   120→    )
   121→    payload["subjective_measures"] = [
   122→        row for row in payload["scorecard_dimensions"] if row.get("subjective")
   123→    ]
   124→    return payload
   125→
   126→
   127→def _emit_requested_output(
   128→    args,
   129→    payload: dict,
   130→    items: list[dict],
   131→) -> bool:
   132→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   133→    if output_file:
   134→        if next_output_mod.write_output_file(
   135→            output_file,
   136→            payload,
   137→            len(items),
   138→            safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   139→            colorize_fn=colorize,
   140→        ):
   141→            return True
   142→        raise SystemExit(1)
   143→
   144→    output_format = getattr(args, "format", "terminal")
   145→    if next_output_mod.emit_non_terminal_output(output_format, payload, items):
   146→        return True
   147→    return False
   148→
   149→
   150→def _plan_queue_context(
   151→    *,
   152→    state: dict,
   153→    plan_data: dict | None,
   154→) -> tuple[float | None, QueueBreakdown | None]:
   155→    plan_start_strict = get_plan_start_strict(plan_data)
   156→    try:
   157→        breakdown = plan_aware_queue_breakdown(state, plan_data)
   158→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
   159→        breakdown = None
   160→    return plan_start_strict, breakdown
   161→
   162→
   163→def _merge_potentials_safe(raw_potentials: dict | None) -> dict | None:
   164→    try:
   165→        return merge_potentials(raw_potentials) or None
   166→    except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError):
   167→        return raw_potentials or None
   168→
   169→
   170→def _get_items(args, state: dict, config: dict) -> None:
   171→    count = getattr(args, "count", 1) or 1
   172→    scope = getattr(args, "scope", None)
   173→    status = getattr(args, "status", "open")
   174→    group = getattr(args, "group", "item")
   175→    explain = bool(getattr(args, "explain", False))
   176→    cluster_arg = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
   177→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
   178→
   179→    target_strict = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
   180→
   181→    # Load the living plan
   182→    plan = load_plan()
   183→    plan_data: dict | None = None
   184→    if (
   185→        plan.get("queue_order")
   186→        or plan.get("overrides")
   187→        or plan.get("clusters")
   188→    ):
   189→        plan_data = plan
   190→
   191→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster if set and no explicit scope/cluster
   192→    effective_cluster = _resolve_cluster_focus(
   193→        plan_data,
   194→        cluster_arg=cluster_arg,
   195→        scope=scope,
   196→    )
   197→
   198→    queue = build_work_queue(
   199→        state,
   200→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   201→            count=count,
   202→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   203→            scope=scope,
   204→            status=status,
   205→            include_subjective=True,
   206→            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
   207→            explain=explain,
   208→            plan=plan_data,
   209→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
   210→            cluster=effective_cluster,
   211→        ),
   212→    )
   213→    items = queue.get("items", [])
   214→
   215→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   216→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   217→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   218→        state,
   219→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="next", plan=plan_data),
   220→    )
   221→
   222→    payload = _build_next_payload(
   223→        queue=queue,
   224→        items=items,
   225→        state=state,
   226→        narrative=narrative,
   227→        plan_data=plan_data,
   228→    )
   229→    write_query(payload)
   230→
   231→    if _emit_requested_output(args, payload, items):
   232→        return
   233→
   234→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   235→    findings_scoped = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(
   236→        state.get("findings", {}),
   237→        state.get("scan_path"),
   238→    )
   239→
   240→    # Extract frozen plan-start score and queue breakdown for lifecycle display
   241→    plan_start_strict, breakdown = _plan_queue_context(
   242→        state=state,
   243→        plan_data=plan_data,
   244→    )
   245→    queue_total = breakdown.queue_total if breakdown else 0
   246→
   247→    next_render_mod.render_queue_header(queue, explain)
   248→    strict_score = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   249→    if next_render_mod.show_empty_queue(
   250→        queue,
   251→        strict_score,
   252→        plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   253→        target_strict=target_strict,
   254→    ):
   255→        return
   256→
   257→    raw_potentials = state.get("potentials", {})
   258→    potentials = _merge_potentials_safe(raw_potentials)
   259→    next_render_mod.render_terminal_items(
   260→        items, dim_scores, findings_scoped, group=group, explain=explain,
   261→        potentials=potentials, plan=plan_data,
   262→        cluster_filter=effective_cluster,
   263→    )
   264→    next_render_mod.render_single_item_resolution_hint(items)
   265→    next_render_mod.render_followup_nudges(
   266→        state,
   267→        dim_scores,
   268→        findings_scoped,
   269→        strict_score=strict_score,
   270→        target_strict_score=target_strict,
   271→        queue_total=queue_total,
   272→        plan_start_strict=plan_start_strict,
   273→        breakdown=breakdown,
   274→    )
   275→    print()
   276→
   277→
   278→__all__ = ["_low_subjective_dimensions", "cmd_next"]
   279→

1→"""Plan move subcommand handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    10→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    11→from desloppify.engine.plan import append_log_entry, load_plan, move_items, save_plan
    12→
    13→
    14→def _resolve_target(plan: dict, target: str | None, position: str) -> str | None:
    15→    """Resolve a cluster name used as a before/after target to a member ID."""
    16→    if target is None:
    17→        return None
    18→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    19→    if target not in clusters:
    20→        return target
    21→    member_ids = clusters[target].get("finding_ids", [])
    22→    if not member_ids:
    23→        return target
    24→    order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    25→    member_set = set(member_ids)
    26→    ordered = [fid for fid in order if fid in member_set]
    27→    if not ordered:
    28→        return member_ids[0]
    29→    return ordered[0] if position == "before" else ordered[-1]
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_move(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Move findings to a position in the queue."""
    34→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    35→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    36→        return
    37→
    38→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    39→    position: str = getattr(args, "position", "top")
    40→    target: str | None = getattr(args, "target", None)
    41→
    42→    if position in ("before", "after") and target is None:
    43→        print(colorize(f"  '{position}' requires --target (-t). Example: plan move <pat> {position} -t <id>", "red"))
    44→        return
    45→    if position in ("up", "down") and target is None:
    46→        print(colorize(f"  '{position}' requires --target (-t) with an integer offset. Example: plan move <pat> {position} -t 3", "red"))
    47→        return
    48→
    49→    plan = load_plan()
    50→
    51→    target = _resolve_target(plan, target, position)
    52→
    53→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    54→    if not finding_ids:
    55→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    56→        return
    57→
    58→    offset: int | None = None
    59→    if position in ("up", "down") and target is not None:
    60→        try:
    61→            offset = int(target)
    62→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
    63→            print(colorize(f"  Invalid offset: {target}", "red"))
    64→            return
    65→        target = None
    66→
    67→    count = move_items(plan, finding_ids, position, target=target, offset=offset)
    68→    append_log_entry(
    69→        plan, "move", finding_ids=finding_ids, actor="user",
    70→        detail={"position": position, "target": target, "offset": offset},
    71→    )
    72→    save_plan(plan)
    73→    print(colorize(f"  Moved {count} item(s) to {position}.", "green"))
    74→
    75→
    76→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_move", "_resolve_target"]
    77→
```

> AGENT

Migration looks correct. Now I need to identify the ACTUAL scenario where this goes wrong. Let me think about this differently.

Given the user says the output shows `commit [blocked]` as item #1, there are two possible root causes:

1. The `queue_order` has synthesis IDs in wrong order (possible through `plan move` or some edge case)
2. The `queue_order` has them in correct order but something downstream reorders them

For case (2), we've seen that `collapse_clusters` only re-sorts when there are clusters to collapse, and the interleave guardrail preserves workflow item order. So case (2) is unlikely.

For case (1), there's NO guard in `apply_plan_order` that says "synthesis items must respect dependency order regardless of queue_order." This is the fundamental design flaw.

But there's an even more fundamental issue: **regardless of how `queue_order` got into a wrong state, `apply_plan_order` should not allow a blocked item to appear before its unblocker in the output.** And the `next` command should not display a blocked item as the #1 recommendation without skipping to the first actionable item.

Interesting! When `finding::abc` is first in queue_order, `apply_plan_order` puts it at position 1, pushing synthesis::observe to position 2. The `item_sort_key` had correctly put observe at position 1 (tier -2 sorts before tier 1), but `apply_plan_order` overrides that with the plan's explicit ordering.

This is by design for regular findings, but it means that if the user or system places findings before synthesis items in queue_order, the synthesis items get demoted. The synthesis items would still appear in the correct relative order though.

There it is. When there are no subjective items, the interleave guardrail early-returns without prepending workflow items. So in that case, `apply_plan_order`'s ordering is the final ordering, and findings can appear before workflow items.

But that's not the user's actual complaint. They're seeing `commit` before `observe`. Let me also check: the interleave is only run when `should_interleave` is True. Let me look at the conditions:

```python
should_interleave = (
    resolved_options.include_subjective
    and status in {"open", "all"}
    and not resolved_options.chronic
    and not resolved_options.scope
    and not resolved_options.cluster
)
```

If the user passes `--scope` or `--cluster`, interleave is skipped! Also, `should_interleave` could be False even with defaults if the conditions aren't met.

But the core problem the user reports -- `commit` appearing before `observe` -- must come from `apply_plan_order` following a `queue_order` that has them in the wrong order, OR from the items not being in `queue_order` at all and ending up in `remaining_existing` which preserves the pre-sort order... wait, but pre-sort order IS correct (from `item_sort_key`).

1. There are no auto-clusters to trigger the re-sort, OR
2. The interleave guardrail doesn't run (no subjective items, or scope/cluster filter is set)

> TOOL

tool_result Bash
```
The confirmed set checks for KEYS in synthesis_stages dict.
When a stage is recorded (e.g., observe), a key "observe" is added to synthesis_stages.
So confirmed = {"observe"} means observe is skipped by build_synthesis_stage_items.

BUT: build_synthesis_stage_items checks if name is in confirmed,
and confirmed is the set of keys in synthesis_stages.
Recording a stage adds the key to synthesis_stages.
So after recording observe, it IS in confirmed and gets skipped.
```

OK, I've now done a very thorough investigation. Let me now summarize the complete diagnosis clearly.

The fundamental issue has TWO independent problems:

**Problem 1**: `apply_plan_order` blindly follows `queue_order` without respecting synthesis stage dependency constraints. If `queue_order` ever gets synthesis IDs in the wrong order (through `plan move`, manual edits, or any future code path), blocked stages will appear before unblocked ones.

**Problem 2**: Even when `item_sort_key` correctly sorts blocked stages after unblocked ones, `apply_plan_order` overrides this with the `queue_order` ordering. And the downstream "rescue" mechanisms (collapse_clusters re-sort and interleave guardrail prepend) don't reliably fire:
- `collapse_clusters` only re-sorts when auto-clusters exist
- The interleave guardrail only runs when both subjective AND objective items exist, and no scope/cluster filter is set

Let me also verify that `next` with `count=1` (default) simply takes `items[0]` -- the first item in the final ordered list.

Looking at `core.py` line 202:
```python
if resolved_options.count is not None and resolved_options.count > 0:
    all_items = all_items[: resolved_options.count]
```

Yes, with `count=1`, it takes just `items[0]`, which is whatever ended up first after all the ordering steps.

Now let me formulate the recommended fix. Let me look at the two approaches the user asked about:

**Option A: Blocked items sort AFTER unblocked ones in `apply_plan_order`.**

This would mean adding a post-sort in `apply_plan_order` that re-sorts synthesis/workflow items by their blocked status. But this is tricky because `apply_plan_order`'s whole purpose is to respect the user's explicit ordering.

**Option B: `next` skips blocked items and shows the first actionable one.**

This is cleaner because:
1. The queue ordering stays as-is (user's plan order is respected)
2. The `next` command simply finds the first unblocked item to display
3. The blocked items are still in the queue but not shown as "next"

Actually, the cleanest fix is **a combination**: fix the sort so blocked synthesis stages always come after their unblocked dependencies, regardless of queue_order. This should only apply to synthesis/workflow stages (not regular findings), because the dependency relationship is a hard constraint, not a preference.

Let me also consider: the `item_sort_key` already handles this correctly. The problem is that `apply_plan_order` overrides it. The fix should be applied in `apply_plan_order` specifically for synthesis/workflow stages with dependency constraints.
